            FINDING #15

(106-110)   The FLORIDA System's "alert" generation process, used to notify
            case workers of required actions, was not operating efficiently
            as evidenced by the mass accumulation of undeleted alerts,
            thereby limiting the department's assurances that its programs
            were administered in accordance with controlling laws,
            administrative rules, and grantor restrictions. Furthermore,
            applicable user group review and approval of the department's
            mass purges of the undeleted alerts was not adequately
            documented, thereby limiting the department's assurance that the
            correct deletions were made.

            RECOMMENDATION #15A & B

            The department implement measures to ensure that any subsequent
            mass changes of data, such as the purge of undeleted alerts in
            July and August 1992, be undertaken only after documented
            approval of the planned changes by users as well as the
            department's electronic data processing staff. Documentation
            shoulda also be maintained to evidence approval by the affected
            user groups of the results of such changes. We also recommend
            that the department continue their efforts to examine the alert
            generation process for the purpose of ensuring timely case
            actions, while at the same time maximizing computer resources and
            related response times.

            MANAGEMENT’S RESPONSE #15A

            We concur.      Management Systems staff will implement a formal
            sign-off procedure including data processing and user staff, with
            appropriate retention of the sign-off documents for future mass
            purges    of data.


            MANAGEMENT’S RESPONSE #15B

            We concur. The FLORIDA Steering Committee has convened a focus
            group to review the alerts generation process, and recommend
            application functionality changes to reduce the overall number of
            alerts. They will also make recommendations for continued
            periodic purges of certain types of alerts.

            recoupment activities.

                 The department enact corrective measures to ensure timely and
                 periodic reconciliation of eligibility records between the

                 timely basis      in the   future.

                 and FMMIS are presently being run monthly.  One more monthly

   ae            then be run quarterly.



                 FINDING    #21


(134-138)         Programming changes made during nightly processing of data were
                  not adequately controlled to ensure that they were documented and
                  timely reviewed by applicable management staff and/or
                  acceptance/user      groups.


                  RECOMMENDATION #21

                  The department establish the following corrective measures:

                  e   To the extent practicable,         implement software that provides a
                      secured record of all changes made by programmers to programs
                      in the fix and override libraries.  Such records should not be
                      available to the programmers making those changes, and should
                      be used by supervisors and acceptance/user group in reviewing
                      and approving changes.

                  e   Implement reasonable time standards in which changes made to
                      programs in the fix and override libraries must be reviewed,
                      deleted,    corrected,     approved and/or placed back into the
                      production libraries.  We noted that the department has
                      initiated a plan to implement such standards.  That plan
                      should be carried out.


                  MANAGEMENT’S RESPONSE #21


                  We concur and have a procedure in place for documenting the
                  justification for use of fix and override libraries, and removing
                  them as soon as possible.           In December 1992 there were 814
                  entries in the override library; this precipitated a concerted
                  effort to reduce that number and as of April 1992,                 there are 290

Report
 Par.

— Ho.            FINDING   #24

(1507153)        Employees responsible for determining applicant/client
                 eligibility for benefits through the FLORIDA System, performed
                 and/or had the capability to perform incompatible duties, thereby
                 increasing the risk of undetected errors and irregularities.

                 RECOMMENDATION #24

                 The department implement appropriate measure to ensure that,              to
                 the extent practicable, individual employees do not perform
                 incompatible duties.  Specifically, the functions of entering
                 initial client information, processing the cases for eligibility
                 and benefit level determinations, and approving/authorizing the
                 cases for benefits should be segregated among different
                 employees.      Additionally revisions made by case workers to client
                 information such as name, addresses, etc., should be
                 reviewed/authorized by appropriate supervisory personnel.

                 MANAGEMENT’S RESPONSE #24

                 We concur and have established an employee fraud prevention task
                 force to review policies, procedures and system functionality
                 with emphasis on proactively improving security within existing
                 resources. The Office of the Auditor General, Division of Public
                 Assistance Fraud (DPAF) was included in the initial meeting of
                 this task force to provide input on security and assist with the
                 development of prevention strategies.  The department has also
                 requested funding for a position to further enhance the fraud
                  prevention activities already underway within the programs.
                  Grant Thornton will be assisting us in identifying controls to
                  prevent errors and irregularities as well.

                  We concur with the recommendation that supervisory personnel
                  review changes as they are processed by field staff through the
                  case review process.       We recently began supervisory case reading
                  as a part of the ongoing quality assurance process.               In January
                  1993 we developed our quality improvement reinvestment plan with
                  the Food Stamp regional and national office.   This plan
                  establishes 22 Human Services Analyst positions to conduct random
                  sample reviews of our service centers in the six targeted
                  counties.   These individuals will provide continual feedback to
                  the operational management in the districts.               Clearly we will
                  then have statistically valid data on a service center and
                  possibly unit basis.

                  This random case review process should also assist in some of the
                  concerns regarding security since the integrity of the caseload
                  will be assessed continually.  If problems of this nature occur
   aL             they should be detectable by management.

— ND          FINDING    #25

(154-157) _   The department did not maintain and retain adequate computer logs
              documenting all significant events and actions relating to the
              FLORIDA System's equipment, software, data base, tuning
              activities, and operations; thereby limiting management's
              capability to review System changes and identify or ensure timely
              resolution of System problems and inefficiencies.

              RECOMMENDATION #25

              The department establish procedures providing for the logging of
              all significant events relative to the FLORIDA System's
              operations, equipment, software, data bases, and tuning
              activities. Retention periods for those logs should be adequate
              to allow for appropriate managerial review.

              MANAGEMENT’S RESPONSE #25

              We concur.        Management Systems staff have implemented the
              maintenance of logs which document significant events and actions
                                                                             ,
              relating to changes in the FLORIDA Systems equipment, software
              data base, tuning activities, and operations. However, these
              logs reside in different forms in different documents. This
              allows each technical unit to construct and use documents that
              optimize their different update procedures and reporting needs.
              Management Systems staff are reviewing the capability of the
              software product, Information/Management, to track more of the
              change    data.




              FINDING #26


(158-161)     Adequate managerial reviews were not conducted of the use of tothe
              department's software established for making direct updates
              the FLORIDA System's production data. The lack of such reviews
              increases the risk of unauthorized modifications not being
              detected in a timely manner.

              RECOMMENDATION #26

              The department establish and implement procedures providing for
              managerial reviews of the DLT/O software's use. Such reviews
              should be conducted by knowledgeable managerial staff who are
              independent of the programmers initiating and authorizing the
              software's use.

 TO:            District Economic Services Program Offices

 FROM: VA” pon Winstead, Assistant Secretary (PDES)
         vs    Xv

 SUBJECT:       March Status Report on the "Blueprint for
                    Improved Client Services"


      Attached is the Blueprint for Improved Client Services
 which contains the status update as of 3/31/93. Several of
 the action steps listed in the Blueprint are on target and
 have been completed.  Congratulations to all staff for your
 outstanding effort in converting 100% of the Food Stamp and
 AFDC cases!

      In addition to this remarkable achievement, several other
 steps of the Blueprint deserve recognition for their success.
 The Blueprint training developed by district and headquarters
 staff has received many positive comments from those who have
 attended the sessions. Training is continuing throughout the
 state and all staff are urged to make this a part of their
 schedule. Monitoring is underway in all districts and is
 getting workers and supervisors on the quality case work
 track.                                           .


         A step has been added to goal A7 to increase the
 awareness of Quality Control by developing and providing
 training to all levels of staff over the next few months. The
 training will be developed jointly with QC and Economic
 Services and will be presented by district program office be
 staff and the local QC supervisors. Further details will.
 forthcoming.

       Please share the Blueprint with all appropriate staff and
  feel free to contact Bill Majorossy at Suncom 277-4390 should
 you have any questions or concerns.
